class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives 2022 reported cadmium at 13.4 µg/kg (mean) in baby Cereals Dry, as sold from GEMS/Food contaminants database.<sup class="cite">[[sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Cadmium: dietary exposure assessment</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">WHO Food Additives Series, No. 82 (Safety evaluation of certain contaminants in food, prepared by the 91st meeting of JECFA)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) 2014 reported inorganic arsenic at 15.3 µg/kg (mean) in baby Cereals Dry, as sold from European dietary-survey occurrence data.<sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

For a buyer the practical points are the form bought, the basis a result is reported on, where the baby Cereals Dry came from and the laboratory’s reporting limit. Testing finds contamination but does not remove it.

Results stay on the basis each source reports: as sold or fresh weight, dry weight, or unstated. Dry-weight, as-sold and unstated results are never pooled, and no moisture conversion is applied.

<div class="hmi-analyte-explorer" id="hmi-occurrence-baby-cereals-dry" data-analyte-explorer data-compact-intro="true" data-source-summaries="true"><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure" data-explorer-context><summary data-open-context aria-haspopup="dialog" aria-controls="hmi-occurrence-baby-cereals-dry-drawer">Comparison methods and scope</summary><div data-explorer-context-content>

<p>Each row is one statistic a source reports for one form of baby cereals dry, group and metal; percentiles, ranges and maxima reported for the same samples as a mean or median are shown on that row. Different forms and preparation states are kept apart, and dry-weight results never share a reference with as-sold or fresh-weight results. Ratings place a result against an HMI reference built from monitoring results and ordinary-setting medians for the same form and basis. Same-study contrasts compare a contaminated or treated site with its control, targeted with random sampling, a prepared form with the unprocessed one, or one group of a study with the median of its other groups. Held rows failed a check against their own source and are shown but not compared.</p>

Baby Cereals Dry, as sold; As sold, as eaten or fresh weight, µg/kg; no moisture conversion. Distribution of subgroup median concentration values.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Reference (µg/kg)</th><th scope="col">Q1</th><th scope="col">Median</th><th scope="col">Q3</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>Primary HMI reference</td><td>≤10</td><td>≤10</td><td>≤18</td></tr><tr><td>Alternative weighting</td><td>≤10</td><td>≤10</td><td>≤20</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>92 reference units; 1 sampling family. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>92</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for baby cereals dry, as sold on a as sold, as eaten or fresh weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for baby cereals dry, as sold on this basis in the completed baby cereals dry ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Counts describe evidence units, not independent studies or certainty.</p><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Contributing family</th><th scope="col">Reference units</th><th scope="col">Weight</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>WHO GEMS/Food</td><td>4</td><td>100%</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Methods</p><p>HMI pools subgroup medians for baby cereals dry, as sold on a dry weight basis: monitoring results summarized as one median per reporting region, sampling type and collection year, total diet composites and printed sample values as single observations, and printed subgroup medians from ordinary farms or markets. Each sampling family has equal total weight. Non-detects stay as zero-to-limit intervals, so quartiles carry lower and upper bounds. Means, ranges and maxima never enter as observations. Other forms, other bases, targeted sampling and results from mining, industrial, wastewater-affected, experimental, fertiliser or manufacturer settings are kept out of this reference.</p><p>Inverse weighted empirical CDF; midpoint at exact cumulative-weight boundaries. At least four reference units and a definitely positive Q1 to Q3 spread are required. This is a display eligibility rule, not statistical validation.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Limitations</p><p>WHO GEMS/Food is the only compatible sampling family for baby cereals dry, as sold on this basis in the completed baby cereals dry ledger, so this single-family reference cannot represent other markets. Reporting regions and purchase markets are not growing areas. Non-detects with differing limits widen the bounds. A position is descriptive concentration, not a safety or compliance judgment.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Admitted source families</p><ul><li><a href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" class="internal" data-slug="sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants" data-no-cite-meta>WHO GEMS/Food</a></li></ul><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reference version and interpretation</summary><p>Frozen evidence selection: 2026-10-22. Method: hmi-pooled-reference-v2.</p><p>No admitted reference</p><p>The reference is computed by HMI from admitted evidence, not copied from a study baseline. It describes the evidence currently in this scope; it is not a worldwide or current-lot benchmark. Source, year and region filters do not change it. Different HMI scopes are not one common ranking.</p></details></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Collection period</p><p data-timeline-scope>2011</p><div data-selected-timeline><p>No year-by-year sample register is reconstructed from this summary.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Geographic scope</p><p data-regional-scope>GEMS/Food contaminants database</p><div data-selected-regional><p>All samples in the study.</p></div><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Sampling, analysis and reporting limits</p><p>various national laboratory methods; medium-bound treatment with LOD/2.</p><p>No censoring reported</p><p>Global GEMS/Food records (N=1989; 483 below LOD, 24%); medium-bound mean (non-detects at LOD/2); heavily weighted to WHO European Region data.</p><p class="hmi-drawer-section-title" role="heading" aria-level="3">Shared samples and study overlap</p><p>Subgroup rows from the same source share its laboratory and sampling frame; they are not independent studies.</p><p>Each table row describes a food/analyte result. Repeated rows do not add independent samples or datasets. No cross-study trend is calculated in this comparison mode.</p><details class="hmi-drawer-disclosure"><summary>Reported values, conversion and source locator</summary><div class="hmi-drawer-table"><table><thead><tr><th scope="col">Source statistic</th><th scope="col">Original result</th><th scope="col">Displayed unit conversion</th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td>mean</td><td>13.4 ug/kg (MB mean)</td><td>as recorded in the findings store</td></tr></tbody></table></div><p>Appendix, Global mean cadmium concentrations table.</p><p>No moisture, cooking-yield or species conversion is applied to the original finding above. The HMI reference for lead in baby Cereals Dry, as sold pools 92 subgroup units from 1 sampling family, with a median of below 10 µg/kg and a middle half spanning below 10 (lower quartile) and below 18 µg/kg (upper quartile).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium in baby Cereals Dry, as sold, the reference median was between 5 and 10 µg/kg, with a middle half from between 2.5 and 4 to between 11.8 and 14 µg/kg across 56 units.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span <a id="hmi-ing-024"></a>
<a id="what-costs-constraints-adverse-effects-or-failure-modes-are-documented-and-which-are-unknown"></a>

Metals do not move together, so a lot that passes a lead limit says little about cadmium, arsenic or nickel unless those are tested as well.



<a id="sampling-and-testing"></a>
## Testing baby Cereals Dry for metals

<a id="hmi-ing-025"></a>
<a id="what-sampling-design-is-appropriate-for-the-ingredient-heterogeneity-lot-and-decision"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-026"></a>
<a id="which-methods-species-measurements-validation-evidence-and-reporting-limits-are-needed"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-027"></a>
<a id="what-can-a-certificate-of-analysis-or-non-detect-establish-and-what-remains-untested"></a>

Results depend on the method and its reporting limit. Reporting limits for lead ranged from 2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 4,000 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for cadmium ranged from 2 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 200 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> Reporting limits for total arsenic ranged from 3 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food) to 10,100 µg/kg (WHO GEMS/Food), so a non-detect from a less sensitive method says much less.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

A useful lot result names the form, the basis and a quantification limit well below the limit that applies.



<a id="procurement-and-process-control"></a>
## Buying and verifying baby Cereals Dry

<a id="hmi-ing-028"></a>
<a id="what-origin-traceability-processing-and-analytical-information-should-a-buyer-request"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-029"></a>
<a id="which-control-or-specification-choices-are-supported-and-which-are-policy-choices-requiring-separate-justification"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-030"></a>
<a id="what-supplier-process-or-test-result-changes-should-trigger-investigation-or-requalification"></a>

No maximum level for metals in baby Cereals Dry was verified from primary text for this page; buyers should confirm the limit that applies in the destination market.



<a id="finished-product-and-exposure-implications"></a>
## Exposure from baby Cereals Dry

<a id="hmi-ing-031"></a>
<a id="how-do-inclusion-rate-processing-yield-preparation-and-other-ingredients-affect-a-finished-product-estimate"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-032"></a>
<a id="how-do-consumption-patterns-and-intended-populations-affect-dietary-exposure-interpretation"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-033"></a>
<a id="what-conclusions-require-finished-product-measurements-or-a-separate-exposure-assessment"></a>

No source in the library converted baby Cereals Dry concentrations into a verified estimate of intake for a defined population. Concentrations describe the food, not individual risk.


<a id="why-this-commodity-accumulates-heavy-metals"></a>
<a id="regulatory-limits-that-apply"></a>
<a id="requirements-evidence-assessment-and-updates"></a>
## Evidence review and remaining gaps

<a id="hmi-ing-034"></a>
<a id="which-primary-regulatory-instruments-apply-to-this-exact-ingredient-form-species-market-and-date"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-035"></a>
<a id="what-searches-eligibility-decisions-source-families-and-appraisal-underpin-the-answers-and-certainty-judgments"></a>
<a id="hmi-ing-036"></a>
<a id="what-remains-unresolved-when-was-each-answer-reviewed-and-what-changed-in-this-version"></a>

Every source record that names baby Cereals Dry was decided on its content: 5 occurrence extracted, 5 records in all. The occurrence table renders every extracted result; held values remain visible with their reason.

References identify the sources used in these answers. The Sources index preserves additional discovery links; inclusion there does not mean a source has been reviewed for this assessment. Corrections, completed searches, new direct evidence and changes in requirements should reopen the relevant answer.


<a id="universal-faqs"></a>
## Baby Cereals Dry FAQs

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-baby-cereals-dry" data-anchor="what-heavy-metals-are-found-in-baby-cereals-dry" data-question="What heavy metals are found in baby Cereals Dry?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>What heavy metals are found in baby Cereals Dry?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

The library reports lead, cadmium, total arsenic, inorganic arsenic, dimethylarsinic acid, total mercury, chromium and tin in baby Cereals Dry, most often lead, cadmium and total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022|2]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">Joint FAO/WHO Expert Committee on Food Additives · 2022</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Cadmium: dietary exposure assessment</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">WHO Food Additives Series, No. 82 (Safety evaluation of certain contaminants in food, prepared by the 91st meeting of JECFA)</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/jecfa-91st-cadmium-2022">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa-mercury-methylmercury-2012|4]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">EFSA Panel on Contaminants in the Food Chain (CONTAM) · 2012</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa-mercury-methylmercury-2012">Scientific Opinion on the Risk for Public Health Related to the Presence of Mercury and Methylmercury in Food</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2012;10(12):2985</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa-mercury-methylmercury-2012">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> WHO GEMS/Food submissions reported lead in baby Cereals Dry, as sold from 3,154 results in 4 reporting regions, with subgroup medians from 27 to 275 µg/kg.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-baby-cereals-dry" data-anchor="which-regions-report-the-lowest-levels-in-baby-cereals-dry" data-question="Which regions report the lowest levels in baby Cereals Dry?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>Which regions report the lowest levels in baby Cereals Dry?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Among GEMS/Food reporting regions, baby Cereals Dry, as sold had the lowest lead subgroup median in EMRO (27 µg/kg) and the highest in PAHO (275 µg/kg); reporting regions are markets, not growing areas.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> For cadmium, the lowest regional median was in PAHO (3 µg/kg) and the highest in WPRO (31 µg/kg).<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>

<details class="qa" data-hmi-faq="1" id="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-baby-cereals-dry-is-inorganic" data-anchor="how-much-of-the-arsenic-in-baby-cereals-dry-is-inorganic" data-question="How much of the arsenic in baby Cereals Dry is inorganic?">
<summary><span class="mk" aria-hidden="true"></span>How much of the arsenic in baby Cereals Dry is inorganic?</summary>
<div class="ans" data-hmi-faq-answer="1">

Inorganic arsenic, the more toxic form, was measured in 17 results from 2 sources, far fewer than total arsenic.<sup class="cite">[[sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants|1]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">World Health Organization · 2026</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">GEMS/Food Contaminants database heavy-metal exports</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">GEMS/Food Contamination Monitoring and Assessment Programme</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/who-gemsfood-heavy-metal-contaminants">Read source →</a></span></span></sup><sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup> European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) 2014 reported inorganic arsenic at 15.3 µg/kg (mean) in baby Cereals Dry, as sold from European dietary-survey occurrence data.<sup class="cite">[[sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs|3]]<span class="cite-card"><span class="cite-card-head"><span class="cite-card-authors">European Food Safety Authority (EFSA) · 2014</span><a class="cite-card-title" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Dietary exposure to inorganic arsenic in the European population</a></span><span class="cite-card-body"><span class="cite-card-journal">EFSA Journal 2014;12(3):3597</span><a class="cite-card-review" href="../sources/efsa2014-dietary-exposure-iAs">Read source →</a></span></span></sup>

</div>
</details>
